# Blue Bee — CHANGELOG (build log)

The running record of every build on Blue Bee. **Update this after every build — one dated line per change, newest on top.** This is the detail log that lives with the code; material milestones also roll up to the founder's master log at `Projects/CHANGELOG.md` under the tag `[BLUE BEE]`.

Format:

```
## YYYY-MM-DD
- HH:MM IST · what changed · → files/areas touched
```

---

## 2026-09-08
- 04:59 IST · **fix(payments): close the order-level payment race** — `createIntent` now locks the Order row (mirroring the existing invoice-level lock) before deciding whether to reuse a pending payment or create a new one, so two concurrent pay attempts can never create two PENDING rows / orphan one on the `stripePaymentIntentId` unique constraint. → `apps/api/src/payments/payment.service.ts`, `apps/api/test/payments.e2e-spec.ts`
- 14:00 IST · **fix(driver): clear the location outbox on shift end** — a failed-to-send ping from an ended shift could previously sit queued and get flushed (and misattributed) under the NEXT shift once the driver started one; `stopTracking()` now clears the outbox, and a session-token guard in the flush closure stops an in-flight send from posting under a stale shift/vehicle binding. → `apps/driver/src/location/duty-cycle.ts`, `apps/driver/src/location/tracker.ts`
- 15:45 IST · **fix(credits): idempotency-protect the console credit adjustment** — `POST /v1/admin/customers/:id/credits/adjust` now wraps `IdempotencyInterceptor` like every other money-moving mutation in the codebase; a retried/double-clicked finance adjustment now replays the cached response instead of writing a second `CreditEntry`. New `credits.e2e-spec.ts` proves replay-safety and concurrency-safety. → `apps/api/src/credits/credits.module.ts`, `apps/api/src/credits/credits.controller.ts`, `apps/api/test/credits.e2e-spec.ts`

## 2026-08-12
- 18:20 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Developer handover audit — the honest state of the build, verified end to end.** Founder is handing the project to another developer. Produced a single document that answers the two questions asked (*which folder is live?* and *what's pending?*) from evidence, not memory. **(1) Which repo:** `/Volumes/Gowtham/Blue Bee` is the repository; **`bluebee-integration` is a git worktree of it** (`.git` is a 69-byte pointer), pinned to `chore/railway-verify` and ~7 weeks stale — `git worktree list` is the proof. **(2) Git state, flagged as urgent:** the 3 newest commits (`ef3df1f` Royal rebrand + full customer UX redesign, `029ee3a` demo seed, `d759f05` reference-render match) are **committed locally and never pushed** — they exist only on this drive; local `main` is 33 commits behind `origin/main`; the other 13 branches are fully merged and safe to delete. **(3) Build health re-run from scratch:** typecheck **10/10 packages**; unit tests **977/977 across 115 suites** (api 338, customer 229, web 216, driver 49, owner 30, api-client 7, design-tokens 8). The first parallel run showed 44 red — **all timeouts from Jest worker contention, zero assertion failures**; every suite is green under `--runInBand`. This is the second time that trap has cost a diagnosis (see the Inc-38 handoff), so it is now called out twice in the handover. **(4) Live env probed, not assumed:** `/v1/health` 200 with postgis 3.4.3 + timescaledb 2.26.4 + redis; console and `/v1/docs` 200; route probes returned **401 not 404** on `/v1/account/credits` and `/v1/account/referral`, proving the live API is at least Inc-37/38 — which means **`docs/DEPLOY-RAILWAY.md` is stale** (it documents 13 migrations at `792f701`; the repo has 28). **(5) The pending list, verified against code rather than inherited from old docs:** *P0 credentials* — the OTP `devCode` is still returned in the API response under `NODE_ENV=staging` + console SMS (**a full auth bypass in public hands — the single most urgent item**); S3 bucket proven but 4 vars unset; Stripe absent (LocalPaymentProvider, no money path); email/push sandboxed; **no `android.config.googleMaps.apiKey` in ANY of the three `app.json`s** though `react-native-maps` is a dependency in all three — Android maps render blank, which breaks live tracking, the marquee feature; Nominatim geocoding capped at ~1 req/s with the Google provider unimplemented. *P0 build config* — `apps/driver` and `apps/owner` `eas.json` **production** profiles carry no `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL`, so a production build falls back to `localhost:3000`; the identical bug was fixed for `apps/customer` in `f2c52b9` and **never applied to the other two**; owner has no EAS `projectId` at all. *P1 frontend* — enumerated every honest "Coming soon" row and separated the ones with a backend from the ones without: `NotificationPreference` **model + server-side read exist but there is no write endpoint** (cheapest real win); `Claim` **model exists with no service, no controller, no write path, no UI** (PRD A6.2); tip-at-payment and saved payment methods **not built anywhere** (A5.1/A1.2); Apple/Google sign-in verified server-side but `getProviderIdToken` throws on-device. *P2/P3* — no pen test, no load test, no Sentry (zero prod error visibility), console refresh token in `localStorage`, and 0 of 3 apps submitted to any store. Also recorded the 10 gotchas that would otherwise be rediscovered (IPv6-only private net, `PGDATA` subdir, the `railway volume add` CLI panic, build-time env baking, append-only triggers, the still-live pilot owner `+14165550100`). → `handoffs/2026-08-12-developer-handover.md` (new) + a published web version for sharing. The audit itself changed **no source** — but on the founder's instruction the outstanding git state was then **resolved in the same session**: `CLAUDE.md`'s uncommitted Radon screenshot rule committed separately (`e02367e`) so it stayed attributable, the handover committed (`b410ba3`), **`feature/customer-ux` pushed to origin** (the 3 at-risk commits are now off the drive), local `main` fast-forwarded 33 commits to `origin/main`, and **`feature/customer-ux` merged into `main` as `90fa528`** — clean automatic merge, no conflicts, re-verified after the merge (typecheck **10/10**, customer **229/229**). `main` and `origin/main` are now identical at `159c5da`. **Flagged in the handover:** that merge landed the customer UX redesign on `main` **without a second reviewer on the diff** — it is presentation-only by construction (the frozen `src/api` / `src/session` / navigation-param / `src/push` / `src/config` contract is untouched) and green, but `d759f05` and `ef3df1f` should be read before anyone builds on them. The 13 fully-merged stale branches were deliberately **left in place** — that cleanup is the incoming developer's call, not ours.

## 2026-07-13
- 22:15 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-39 — the garment picker is a LIST, not a photo gallery.** Founder, once the garments finally appeared: *"can you make it a list rather than the catalogue? A list will be easier to skim."* Correct — a 2-up grid of photo cards is the right shape for **browsing** and the wrong one for what a customer is actually doing here: running down a known list of their own garments and tapping "+" a few times. Each garment is now **one full-width row — thumbnail · name · price · stepper on a single scannable line** — so 3 garments or 30 read the same way and the thumb never leaves the right edge. The photo survives as a 40px thumbnail (it aids recognition; it just shouldn't dictate the layout), and a category's rows share one bordered container so the group reads as a list rather than N floating cards. **Also fixed while in there:** the picker wrapped itself in a `Card` titled *"Dry cleaning"*, which since Inc-38 sat **inside** the screen's *"Book Dry Cleaning"* card — a card in a card, naming the service twice, and **hard-coding a name the founder owns** in the console. Both gone: the picker no longer names the service at all. **Zero behaviour change** — same `serviceId`s, same server-resolved prices, same per-garment order lines. → `apps/customer/src/components/DrycleanPicker.tsx`. **Tests +2** (a garment renders as a full-width ROW, not a half-width grid cell, with its name + price + stepper all inside that one row; and the picker never names the service itself). **Proof:** typecheck 10/10 · lint 6/6 · customer **225/225**. No deploy needed — presentation-only, and the founder runs the Expo dev client (Metro serves this repo).
- 21:40 IST · **[BLUE BEE] FIX (console) — the catalog price box was an unlabelled sliver, and EVERY fixed-width `Input` in the console was being silently ignored.** Founder: *"can you fix the (box) before the Save — I don't even know what that is, it's too small."* **The root cause was not local to the catalog.** `Input`'s base class starts with `w-full`, `cn()` is a plain string join (no `tailwind-merge`), and Tailwind emits `w-full` **after** the numeric width scale — so `<Input className="w-24" />` never got 24: it stretched to full width and was then crushed to a few pixels by its flex siblings (Save / Deactivate / the reorder arrows). The founder's `$15.00` was *in* the box; there was no room to draw it. **Six call sites** across the console (`WeightPanel`, `CorporateAccounts`, `DispatchBoard`, `ServiceZones`, `CatalogAdmin` ×2) were passing widths that never applied. **FIX:** `ui.tsx` — the control now yields its default `w-full` whenever the caller states a width, so a declared width actually wins (fixes all six, no new dependency). `CatalogAdmin` — the price field now **says "Price"**, shows the **`$`** it is edited in, holds its width, and wraps instead of being squeezed. Money you cannot see is money you cannot trust. → `apps/web/components/{ui.tsx,CatalogAdmin.tsx}`. **Tests +1** (the field is labelled, carries the real base rate, and its declared width survives — `w-28` present, `w-full` absent). **Proof:** typecheck 10/10 · lint 6/6 · web **216/216**. **DEPLOYED to staging** (web `80f682b`).
- 21:05 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-38 — FIX: the founder's dry-clean garments never reached the app. Per-garment booking restored, and made data-driven.** Founder report: *"I added Shirt and Pant in the dry-cleaning catalogue — why is it not reflected in the app? Even if I refresh."* **Not a refresh bug and not a console bug — the app had stopped asking.** `GET /v1/catalog/dryclean` was returning their real, correctly-priced garments the whole time (Shirt $5.00, Coat $15.00, Pant $7.00, each resolved through `PricingService` with corporate overrides). **Inc-36 deleted `DrycleanPicker.tsx` and stopped calling `getDrycleanCatalog()`** as part of "remove the garment sub-catalog" — leaving the client method alive but called by nothing. So no refresh could ever surface them: there was no code path that looked. **The deeper defect it masked:** the Dry Cleaning card is a flat `PER_ITEM` rate, so the app asked "how many items?" and multiplied — **a $15 coat and a $5 shirt would have been charged identically**, and the founder's per-garment prices could never reach a customer. The console was quietly promising prices the app could not honour. **FIX — the catalog decides the flow, not the code.** `HomeServiceCardView` gains `garmentCount` (active per-ITEM services inside the card's child categories), computed server-side. `garmentCount > 0` ⇒ Book drills into the garment picker (each garment its own line at its own price); `0` ⇒ the Inc-36 one-measure flow. **The app never checks for the words "dry cleaning"** — nest garments under any card in the console and that card drills in; remove them and it reverts. `GET /v1/catalog/dryclean?cardId=` now scopes the picker to the tapped card (it previously returned EVERY nested category in the org, so the day a second card got garments, one card's picker would have shown the other's). Booking uses the **exact pre-Inc-36 contract** — one line per garment type, `serviceId` = the garment, qty in `estimatedMeasure` — so the quote path is untouched. Honesty: a per-garment card with no garments listed says *"No garments listed yet"* and **never falls back to a measure box**, which would price a coat like a shirt at a rate the customer never agreed to. → `packages/shared/src/catalog.ts`, `apps/api/src/catalog/{catalog.service,catalog.controller}.ts`, `apps/customer/src/screens/{BookScreen,HomeScreen}.tsx`, `apps/customer/src/components/DrycleanPicker.tsx` (restored), `apps/customer/src/{navigation.ts,api/customer-client.ts}`. **Tests +15** (customer 223/223: garments render at their own prices with no measure box · 2 shirts + 1 coat = **$25.00**, not 3 × a flat rate · books one line per garment carrying each garment's own service · removing the last of a garment drops its line · an empty per-garment card never falls back to a measure box · a non-garment card still uses one-measure and never fetches garments; api e2e: the Home card reports its `garmentCount`, the picker is scoped to that card, and the count equals what the picker can actually book). **Proof:** typecheck 10/10 · lint 6/6 · api-unit 338/338 · customer 223/223 · driver 49/49 · web 215/215 · **full api e2e 466/466 (38 suites) on a fresh seeded DB**. ⚠️ Two earlier e2e runs showed 12 and 2 failures — **both were `beforeAll`/test TIMEOUTS caused by leaked `jest-worker` processes from unit suites I ran concurrently** (load avg 5.65), not defects; killing them and re-running on a clean box gave 466/466. **DEPLOYED to staging** (api `020beb2`): `/v1/catalog/home` now reports `garmentCount: 3` on Dry Cleaning and `0` on every other card, and `/v1/catalog/dryclean?cardId=<dry-cleaning>` returns exactly the founder's garments (Shirt $5.00 · Coat $15.00 · Pant $7.00) while a card with no garments returns an empty picker — no leakage. No EAS build or OTA was needed: the founder views the app through the Expo dev client (Metro serves the JS from the repo), and no native dependency changed.
- 19:20 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-37 — Home tab + REAL referral credit (earn · balance · spend), founder-editable from the console.** The ask was a Home screen ("get free credits", My orders, My account with "your credit") + a place in the web dashboard to edit it. That copy collided head-on with the honesty firewall: **no credit system existed** (Inc-34 deliberately deferred the reward), so shipping the words would have been a hollow promise and a "0 credits" stub. Founder chose *build it for real* — so this increment ships the money, not the marketing. **The ledger** (`CreditEntry`, append-only, signed amounts; balance is `SUM(amount)`, never a stored counter; corrections are new rows): `REFERRAL_EARN` / `REDEMPTION` / `ADJUSTMENT` / `REVERSAL`. Double-spend is impossible **at the database**, not in a service: `@@unique([orgId, kind, referredUserId])` = at most ONE earn per invited friend, ever; `@@unique([orgId, kind, orderId])` = at most ONE redemption per order. **EARN** fires on the real DELIVERED transition in `route-stop.service.ts` (beside the Inc-32 rate-request push, after tx commit) — when a friend you invited has their **first order delivered**, not at signup (farmable). The hook *cannot fail a delivery*: it swallows every error, including the P2002 race. **SPEND** happens in `payment.service.ts#createIntent`: credit is applied before Stripe, the card is charged only the remainder, and when credit covers the whole bill there is **no Stripe intent at all** — `clientSecret: null` (a contract change, `PaymentIntentResponse` also gains `creditApplied` + `orderTotal`), a `method: CREDIT` `SUCCEEDED` Payment, order → PAID. A Stripe-minimum guard holds credit back so the card never sees a sub-50¢ remainder (30.00 balance vs a 30.20 bill → apply 29.70, charge 0.50, the customer keeps 0.30 rather than losing it). Overdraw is refused (`credit.insufficient_balance`). **HONESTY, enforced in code:** a reward only exists if the founder **funds** it (`/content` → Referral reward). With it OFF, `GET /v1/home` returns `reward.enabled: false` and the app renders **no reward line and no balance row at all** — no "0 credits" placeholder, no promise. The reward TERMS are built from config (`"You earn $10.00 credit when a friend you invite has their first order delivered"`), never from a founder's free-text card, so no one can write a promise the ledger won't honour. **HOME** is a 5th tab (Home · Services · Orders · Profile · Refer): "what's new" cards + My orders + My account, **all server-owned** — the app hard-codes zero copy; `HomeBlock` rows (OWNER CRUD at `/v1/admin/home-blocks`, edited at `/content`) are what customers read, and CTAs carry semantic targets (`refer`/`services`/`orders`), never raw routes from a text field. Re-reads on tab focus + pull-to-refresh (the Inc-36a lesson) so a console edit reaches an app that is already open; a failed refresh keeps working content on screen. Console also gains a per-customer **Credits** card (balance + the full append-only ledger + a FINANCE-gated adjustment that *requires* a note). → `prisma/schema.prisma` + migration `20260713120000_credits_and_home_inc37` (CreditEntry, HomeBlock, `PaymentMethod.CREDIT`), `src/credits/*`, `src/home/*`, `logistics/route-stop.service.ts`, `payments/payment.service.ts`, `packages/shared/{credits,home,payments}.ts`, `apps/customer/{App.tsx,screens/DashboardScreen.tsx,screens/PayScreen.tsx}`, `apps/web/{components/HomeContentAdmin.tsx,app/(console)/content/page.tsx,components/CustomerDetail.tsx}`. **Proof:** typecheck 10/10 · lint 6/6 · api-unit 338/338 · customer 209/209 · driver 49/49 · web 215/215 · **full api e2e 465/465 (38 suites) on a fresh seeded DB** — including `credits.e2e` 8/8 (no funded reward ⇒ nothing granted & nothing promised; earn once per friend even across two deliveries + a retry; spend once per order; credit-covers-all charges Stripe nothing; the 50¢ guard; overdraw refused; Home content round-trip) and a new `pod.e2e` case proving the earn fires on a **real driver delivery** (route → POD → complete), stamped with the order that earned it. **DEPLOYED to staging** (commit `94ab4f8` · api + web · the migration auto-applied on boot). The founder's Android build was reporting *"Couldn't load your home — Cannot GET /v1/home"*: that string is Express's default 404, i.e. an **Inc-35 server being asked for an Inc-37 route** — a missing deploy, not a bug (the same build works untouched now). Post-deploy smoke: `/v1/health` ok (postgis 3.4.3 + timescaledb 2.26.4 + redis up); `/v1/home`, `/v1/account/credits`, `/v1/admin/referral-reward`, `/v1/admin/home-blocks` all 401-not-404; `GET /v1/home` as a real customer returns the card + funded reward + her real order + invite code; console `/content` serves the editor (strings present in the shipped bundle). **Founder funded the referral reward at $10.00 CAD** and the "Get free credits" card is live — so the reward line the app renders is now a promise the ledger will honour.
- 15:40 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-36a — FIX: the Home grid never refreshed, so a console catalog edit could not reach an app that was already open.** Found while reviewing the live app: after the catalog was corrected server-side, tapping a service still dead-ended at "Choose a service". **Root cause:** `HomeScreen` fetched `GET /v1/catalog/home` in a single mount `useEffect` — **no `useFocusEffect`, no pull-to-refresh, no re-fetch ever**. React Navigation keeps tabs mounted, so switching tabs never remounts the screen. The app was therefore holding the catalog it fetched at launch (every card `serviceId: null`), and `HomeScreen` only forwards params when `cat.serviceId` is truthy → it navigated to Book with **no service** → the Inc-36 guard rendered the "Choose a service" dead end. A stale grid was silently un-bookable, and every founder rate/description/unit edit in `/catalog` was invisible until the customer killed and relaunched the app — which defeats the console→app round-trip Inc-36 shipped. **FIX** (`HomeScreen.tsx`): re-read the catalog on every tab **focus** (via `navigation.addListener('focus')` — the same NavigationContainer-free pattern BookScreen uses, so it stays unit-testable; the first focus is skipped since mount already loaded), **plus pull-to-refresh** (`RefreshControl` on the grid). A FAILED re-fetch now **keeps the grid on screen** (`hasCatalog` ref) instead of blanking a working catalog into the full-screen error state. **Tests +3** (focus re-fetch flips a `serviceId: null` "Get a quote" card into a priced card that carries its backing service into Book; pull-to-refresh re-reads; a failed refresh preserves the grid). customer **200/200** (23 suites), typecheck + lint clean. **Also applied the catalog fix to STAGING data** via the already-deployed founder endpoint `PATCH /v1/catalog/service-menu/:id` (no deploy, no ssh, no API source shipped — the endpoint already upserts the backing `Service` from a rate): all 8 cards went 0→8 backing services, Wash & Iron `PER_ITEM→PER_KG`, Dry Cleaning `PER_ITEM→PER_LB`, STARTER rates set, **`priceFrom` left NULL so cards still honestly read "Get a quote"** (no unapproved price published). → `apps/customer/src/screens/HomeScreen.tsx` + `HomeScreen.test.tsx`.
- 13:10 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-36 — Simplified customer service booking: one service → one measure → book.** Tapping a Home card now opens a single focused checkout (one measure, live estimate, address, pickup time) instead of a general composer. **typecheck 6/6 · lint 6/6 · customer 197 · driver 49 · web 214 · api-client 7 · api unit 338 · FULL api e2e 456/456 (37 suites) on a FRESH seeded DB.** ⚑ **ROOT CAUSE was not what it looked like.** No enum/unit/migration work was needed — `KG`/`PER_KG`/`PER_LB`/`PER_MEASURE`/`WASHFOLD` **already existed**, and so did the console rate editor (`PATCH /v1/catalog/service-menu/:id`, which already upserts a backing `Service` via the shared `backingServiceUnit()`/`isWeightPricingUnit()`), and the driver weigh→kg-canonical→quote path. The real cause was ONE line — `catalog.service.ts` `serviceId: c.services[0]?.id ?? null`: **none of the 8 Home cards had a backing `Service`** (the founder never entered a rate), so `GET /v1/catalog/home` returned `serviceId: null`, `HomeScreen` navigated `Book` with no params, and BookScreen fell into its **general composer** (item-type chips + service chips + `DrycleanPicker` + a generic size field). That was the complexity on screen. **SEED** (`seed-catalog.ts`): every card now gets a **backing rate `Service`** (deterministic `5e000001-…`) so a tap ALWAYS lands focused; units corrected **Wash & Iron PER_ITEM→PER_KG** and **Dry Cleaning PER_ITEM→PER_LB** (Wash & Fold PER_KG, Rug/Carpet/Upholstery/Blinds FROM_MEASURED/PER_MEASURE unchanged; **`Ironing only` kept PER_ITEM — listed back for founder confirmation, not guessed**); backing unit+vertical derived with the SAME shared helpers the console uses (seed and console can't diverge); **STARTER rates** are configurable placeholders, clearly commented, **NOT official prices** — and **`priceFrom` stays NULL so the grid honestly shows "Get a quote"** until the founder sets a real CAD price (honesty firewall). The `update` branch re-asserts *structure* (unit/model) but **never** `baseRate`/`priceFrom`, so a re-seed can't clobber a founder-set rate. **CUSTOMER APP**: `HomeScreen` no longer renders the `features` process chips ("WASH · TUMBLE-DRY · FOLDED" — field stays in schema/console, just not shown) and the Inc-33 **"Book a pickup" button is REMOVED** (it was the only door into the general composer; the grid IS the picker). `BookScreen` **rewritten**: ONE measure input labelled by the card's `PricingUnit` (kg/lb/sq ft/items — never hardcoded per screen), a **live estimate the moment you type** (no "Add" tap — typing the measure IS the order line), address picker, pickup-slot picker, book. **DELETED**: item-type chips, service chip list, `DrycleanPicker` (component + test — orphaned), the staged-items list. The general composer is now **unreachable**: Book with no service dead-ends back to the grid, and `OrdersScreen`'s CTAs route to the Service grid (`Home` added to `RootStackParamList`, additive). Estimate copy stays honest ("Final quote confirmed after we weigh/measure at pickup"). **WEB + DRIVER: verified, not rebuilt.** **PROOF (real run, no stubs):** all 8 cards return a non-null `serviceId`; **Wash & Fold** 6 kg × $3.50 → app est **$18.90–$24.15**, server `OrderLine.measure=6 rate=3.5000 amount=21.00` + matching `estimateRange`; **Carpet** 40 sqft × $4.00 → **$144–$184**, `measure=40 amount=160.00`; **console round-trip** — `PATCH service-menu {rate 4.25, priceFrom 4.25, description}` → `GET /catalog/home` instantly shows the new copy + "from $4.25 / per kg" and the backing rate goes 3.50→4.25; **driver weigh → authoritative quote** — route → pickup stop → driver weighs **6.40 kg** (append-only, denormalized onto the item) → inspect → INTAKE_INSPECTED → plant confirm → hybrid gate **auto_approved** → **6.4 × $4.25 = $27.20 + $3.54 HST = $30.74**, order **QUOTE_APPROVED** (priced at the founder's CURRENT console rate, not the booking-time rate — server-authoritative). ⚑ **SCOPE NOTE:** this puts the **WASHFOLD** (consumer wash-fold) vertical into the live customer flow, which the PRD defers to a later phase; the enum already existed and **no other Phase-2/3 feature was built**. ⚑ Also fixed **pre-existing** workspace lint debt (driver ChatScreen/WeighScreen, customer ChatScreen/OrderDetailScreen/ReferScreen) — lint was already red before this change. ⚑ `/Volumes/Gowtham/bluebee-integration` **untouched** (stale git worktree of this repo, no HomeScreen at all — a footgun; consider `git worktree remove`). **Not deployed.** → `apps/api/prisma/seed-catalog.ts`, `apps/customer/src/screens/{HomeScreen,BookScreen,OrdersScreen}.tsx` (+ their tests), `apps/customer/src/navigation.ts`, `apps/customer/src/components/DrycleanPicker.tsx` (deleted + test), `apps/driver/src/screens/{ChatScreen,WeighScreen}.tsx`, `apps/customer/src/screens/{ChatScreen,OrderDetailScreen,ReferScreen}.tsx` (lint), `handoffs/2026-07-13-inc36-simplified-service-booking.md`.

Additive migration `20260711000000_ratings_inc32` (CREATE TABLE + indexes + FK, no backfill) hand-written and applied to the dev DB via `prisma migrate deploy` (safe on the shared DB — never resets, only the one pending migration; verified the table/indexes/FK exist). **SHARED** (`ratings.ts`): `createRatingSchema` (score int 1–5, comment ≤1000 trimmed), `patchRatingSchema` (score and/or comment, `comment:null` clears), `RatingView`, `RATING_ERRORS`, and the ONE eligibility definition — `RATEABLE_ORDER_STATUSES` + `isOrderRateable()` (DELIVERED-or-later: DELIVERED/INVOICED/CLAIM_*/CLOSED) — shared by the API gate and the app CTA so they can't disagree. Plus `NOTIFICATION_EVENTS.RATE_REQUEST` and `AccountScorecard.avgRatingScore`. **API** (`ratings/` module): `POST` (`@Roles(RESIDENTIAL_CUSTOMER, ACCOUNT_ADMIN)`, `201`, idempotency-keyed), `PATCH` (same roles), `GET` (+ `OWNER/CSR` for the console). Scope/ownership is the SAME proven `requireOrderForWrite` gate the rest of the order surface uses (cross-account → 403, cross-org/nonexistent → 404 — no existence leak); eligibility → 409 `order_not_rateable` before DELIVERED; one-per-order is DB-enforced → 409 `rating.already_exists`; PATCH is the **original submitter only** (`submittedByUserId`) within a 30-day window (else 403 `rating.not_yours` / 409 `rating.edit_window_closed`). Staff (OWNER/CSR/plant) **cannot** rate (customer-side action). **PUSH.** New `RATE_REQUEST` template + matrix row (`PUSH, EMAIL`, non-urgent) + `NotificationService.dispatchRateRequest` — mirrors `dispatchPayment` exactly (resolve owning-customer recipients → §17.2 matrix → preference/quiet-hours decision → ONE idempotent outbox row per recipient×channel, source = the Order), fully isolated so a push hiccup can never fail the delivery. ⚑ **Fired from the DELIVERED transition but AFTER the tx commits, not inside it:** `deliveryCompleteLeg` (Inc-31's clean slot) now RETURNS the delivered order id, and `run()` enqueues the push after the `$transaction` (exactly where the realtime teardown already sits) — so a rolled-back delivery never fires a push, and the engine's own-connection insert is never orphaned outside the tx. `LogisticsModule` imports `NotificationsModule` (one-directional → no cycle); `DELIVERY_KINDS` reused from Inc-31's `stop-kinds.ts`. **ANALYTICS.** `analytics.service.ts:427` `csat: deferred(...)` → a real `metric<number>()` averaging `score` over ratings in the window (org-scoped `db.rating.aggregate`); `accountScorecard()` gains a real `avgRatingScore` tile (per-account via `order: { accountId }`), surfaced in the web `AccountDetail` scorecard via the existing `Stat` (honours the `Metric` wrapper — a real value, or a computed "no data"/error, **never** the Phase-2 badge and never faked). **CUSTOMER APP** (all additive — the frozen `UX-REDESIGN-BRIEF.md §1` contract is honoured, no existing `client.*` signature or nav param shape changed): `submitRating`/`updateRating`/`getRating`; `rating` added to `FULL_INCLUDE` **and** the order-list include (so a card shows a score or the CTA in one round-trip); `OrderDetail`/`OrderSummary` types + `src/orders/rating.ts` (`isRateable`/`alreadyRated`, wrapping the shared helper); a new `RateScreen` (a `StarRating` 1–5 selector + optional comment + submit; read-only when rated, Edit while in-window); a "Rate this order" CTA on `OrderDetailScreen` right after the timeline (or the submitted score read-only) and a rate/score affordance on the delivered `OrderCard`; `Rate` registered in BOTH `navigation.ts` `RootStackParamList` and `App.tsx` `RootNavParamList` + `<RootStack.Screen>`. **⚑ TEST NOTES (honest coverage on a persistent shared dev DB).** (1) The **PATCH-not-yours** 403 is proven by seeding a rating whose `submittedByUserId` is a different user, because the seed has no two rating-eligible users on one account (each residential customer owns their own account; Account B has a single ACCOUNT_ADMIN). (2) The **account-scorecard average** is asserted EXACTLY (5+3 → 4.0, rowCount 2) on a **fresh account created in the test**, so accumulated ratings from prior runs can't skew it; the **org-wide commercial CSAT** is asserted real-and-sane (status ok, a 1–5 mean, rowCount ≥ what we added) rather than an exact value it can't own. (3) The **RATE_REQUEST enqueue** is asserted in `pod.e2e` (the real POD-gated DELIVERED path) — a `rating.request` Notification for the order's owning customer; the channel-suppression path is the SAME proven `decideChannel` the geofence/payment alerts use. **VERIFY.** shared rebuilt; api/web/customer `tsc` clean; new `ratings.e2e-spec.ts` **6/6** (create+embed, eligibility 409, dup 409 + idempotent replay, PATCH scope 403, cross-account 403 / cross-org 404 / staff 403, analytics avg); `pod.e2e` extended (RATE_REQUEST) + `analytics.e2e` csat/avgRating assertions flipped deferred→real; **FULL api e2e 426 → 432/432 (35 suites)**, web **213/213** (+ `AccountDetail`/`AnalyticsHome` avgRating fixtures), customer **180/180** (+ `RateScreen.test` 4, `OrderDetailScreen.test` +3 CTA cases) — **zero regressions**. **Not committed, NOT deployed** — batches with Inc-28/29/30/31 for ONE `railway up` (api+web; migrations run at boot via `prisma migrate deploy`) + ONE customer EAS rebuild, after the whole arc is audited. ## 2026-07-10
- 23:55 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-31 — Delivery legs: the delivery half of the §5.1 spine (`READY → SCHEDULED_DELIVERY → DELIVERY_EN_ROUTE → DELIVERED`) is wired one-for-one to the pickup legs, POD-gated on BOTH entry points, writing the `DELIVERED` custody chain and making the already-present `DELIVERED → INVOICED` payment path live. FULL api e2e 418 → 426/426 (34 suites, all green); api `tsc` clean. No lifecycle edits — every edge was already in `SPECIALTY_ORDER_TRANSITIONS`.** **⚑ A REAL POD HOLE FOUND AND CLOSED — the reason this wasn't a pure copy of the pickup legs.** `route-stop.service.ts:run()` is the shared applier behind TWO callers: the driver duty-loop (`/v1/driver/stops/:id/complete`) and the ops/dispatch (`/v1/route-stops/:id\:complete`, `@Roles(OWNER, DISPATCHER)`). The POD requirement lived ONLY in the driver layer (`driver.service.ts:226`), and that file's own comment said so out loud: *"Ops `/v1/route-stops` keeps its admin-override complete, ungated."* Before Inc-31 that was harmless — the ops complete only ran `pickupCompleteLeg`, which no-ops on a delivery stop. The moment I wired `deliveryCompleteLeg` into the shared `run()`, the ops path could drive a delivery stop to `DELIVERED` **with no proof of delivery at all**. So the POD assertion is now **moved into the shared path**: `run()` checks for a captured `ProofOfDelivery` before the tx whenever a `DELIVERY/BOTH` stop is completing, from ANY caller — a clean 400 `POD_REQUIRED`, machine untouched. The driver layer keeps its own pre-check (a slightly earlier 4xx), so both paths 400 identically; the e2e asserts BOTH. To stop the two layers ever disagreeing on "what's a delivery stop", the `DELIVERY_KINDS = {DELIVERY, BOTH}` set that was private to `driver.service.ts` is **promoted to `logistics/stop-kinds.ts`** and imported by both. **THE WIRING (mirrors pickup exactly).** (1) **Scheduling** — the publish loop (`route.service.ts`) already advanced pickups `REQUESTED → SCHEDULED_PICKUP`; it now also advances a `DELIVERY/BOTH` stop whose order is `READY` → `SCHEDULED_DELIVERY`, atomic + audited (`reason: 'scheduled onto a published route'`). The per-order dedup set is re-keyed **per `(orderId, leg)`** so a `BOTH` stop can schedule the leg its order-state is at without the pickup key swallowing the delivery one — and never both legs for one order in a single publish (an order is in exactly one state). (2) **Depart** — new `deliveryDepartLeg` mirrors `pickupDepartLeg`: a `DELIVERY/BOTH` stop with its order in `SCHEDULED_DELIVERY` → `DELIVERY_EN_ROUTE`. (3) **Complete** — new `deliveryCompleteLeg` mirrors `pickupCompleteLeg`: order `DELIVERY_EN_ROUTE → DELIVERED`, then a real append-only `CustodyEvent(stage=DELIVERED, deviceId='route-stop-delivery')` **per item in the Inc-29 `ITEM_ORDER_BY` sequence** + `Item.status = DELIVERED`. Both legs are called from `run()` alongside the pickup legs and **self-guard on kind + order-state**, so a PICKUP stop skips the delivery legs and vice-versa, and a `BOTH` stop fires exactly the one leg its order-state allows. **⚑ `Item.status` is set UNCONDITIONALLY to DELIVERED** (no guard on a prior `READY`): per the Inc-30 audit there is no Item state machine and items sit at `INSPECTED`/`RECEIVED` at delivery time — the delivered order is the authoritative signal that each of its items is delivered. (4) **Payment path goes live with no code change** — `payment.service.ts:maybeAdvanceToInvoiced` already does `DELIVERED → INVOICED` on a confirmed payment; it was dead because nothing reached `DELIVERED`. It now fires. (5) **Realtime teardown** unchanged — `run()` already closes the customer tracking channel on any terminal stop; delivery stops use the same terminal path. **No app change** — the driver already drives `depart/arrive/start/complete` + POD capture (delivery stops use the same verbs), and the customer `StatusTimeline` already renders `DELIVERED` (idx 12); both light up now that the status is real. **TESTS.** Extended `pod.e2e-spec.ts` (real app + MinIO + Postgres): a NEW multi-item order is driven the whole real spine to `READY` (through Inc-30: `start-cleaning → send-to-qc → qc pass`), routed as a `DELIVERY` stop, then — publish → assert `Order.status = SCHEDULED_DELIVERY`; depart → `DELIVERY_EN_ROUTE`; complete **without** POD → 400 `POD_REQUIRED` on the driver path **AND** on the dispatch path (asserting the machine is untouched — stop still `IN_PROGRESS`, order still `DELIVERY_EN_ROUTE`); capture real POD → complete → `RouteStop.status = COMPLETED` + `Order.status = DELIVERED` + a `CustodyEvent(DELIVERED)` for **every** item walked in `ITEM_ORDER_BY` order (exactly one each, `deviceId='route-stop-delivery'`) + every `Item.status = DELIVERED`. The existing POD/idempotency/cross-driver-403/PICKUP-unaffected cases stay green (their orders are `REQUESTED`, so the new delivery legs correctly no-op on them). **⚑ The pay→INVOICED proof lives in `payments.e2e-spec.ts`, not `pod.e2e` — and here's the honest why:** `pod.e2e` boots via `createRealtimeTestApp`, which does **not** enable `rawBody`, and the signed Stripe webhook needs the raw bytes to verify — so a webhook fired there would 400 on signature. Rather than mutate the shared realtime factory (already carrying another increment's in-flight edits), the assertion goes where `rawBody` + the `StripeEventVerifier` already live: a DELIVERED order (now a genuinely reachable, payable state) creates a payment-intent, a signed `payment_intent.succeeded` confirms it → `Order.status = INVOICED` + a PAID invoice + mirrored `paymentStatus`. Together `pod.e2e` proves the real path TO `DELIVERED` and `payments.e2e` proves `DELIVERED → INVOICED` — the seam between them is the same `OrderStateMachine`. **VERIFY.** api `tsc` clean; `packages/shared` unchanged (no schema/lifecycle edits — the stop-kinds constant is api-internal); **FULL api e2e 418 → 426/426** (34 suites, `--runInBand --forceExit`), incl. `logistics`/`driver`/`orders`/`tracking`/`recurring` — **zero regression**. **Not committed, NOT deployed** — batched with Inc-28/29/30 (+Inc-32) for one `railway up` at the end of Step 3; no EAS app rebuild needed for this wiring. **Next:** Inc-32 (ratings/CSAT) — gates on the now-real `DELIVERED`, and adds the `RATE_REQUEST` push at the `deliveryCompleteLeg` call site. · → apps/api/src/logistics/{stop-kinds.ts (new), route.service.ts, route-stop.service.ts}, apps/api/src/driver/driver.service.ts, apps/api/test/{pod.e2e-spec.ts, payments.e2e-spec.ts}, CHANGELOG.md, handoffs/2026-07-10-inc31-delivery-legs.md
- 23:30 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-30 — Production advance: the plant half of the §5.1 spine (`QUOTE_APPROVED → IN_CLEANING → QC → READY`, plus the `REWASH` branch) that shipped as DEAD ENUM is now wired, console-driven, and proven end-to-end against the real seeded Postgres. api e2e +6 (`production.e2e-spec.ts` 6/6); web +9 (`ProductionActions.test.tsx` 9/9); `orders.e2e` 17/17 unchanged (no regression); `tsc` clean across api / web / shared.** **THE GAP (confirmed before writing a line).** The brief's central finding held: **no service anywhere in `apps/api/src` ever moved an order past `QUOTE_APPROVED`** — `SPECIALTY_ORDER_TRANSITIONS` (`lifecycle.ts:34-39`) and `OrderStateMachine` already *permit* `IN_CLEANING→QC→READY` + `IN_CLEANING⇄REWASH`/`QC→REWASH`, but nothing *called* `apply()` toward them (the only forward writes were pickup/intake/quote/hold; the `DELIVERED→INVOICED` read in `payment.service.ts` fired on a status no row ever had). So "deliver, then rate" (Inc-31/32) had no reachable start. This increment wires the transitions **only** — zero lifecycle edits, all target states already exist in the enum, nothing invented. **WHAT SHIPPED.** New `ProductionService` (mirrors `IntakeService`: resolve+scope via `requireOrderForWrite`, then ONE audited `OrderStateMachine.apply` inside a `$transaction`) with `startCleaning`/`sendToQc`/`recordQc`/`rewash`; four `@Roles(OWNER, CSR, PLANT_OP)`, idempotency-keyed (class interceptor), `@HttpCode(200)` endpoints on `OrdersController` — `POST /v1/orders/:id/{start-cleaning,send-to-qc,qc,rewash}`; shared Zod schemas + DTOs in `orders.ts`; console `ProductionActions` (offers ONLY the legal next action for the current status, so an illegal call is never issued from the UI; a 409 surfaces as "this order just moved on — refresh") wired into `OrderDetail` beside `OrderActions`, plus the four client methods and `can.production` / `PRODUCTION_ROLES` mirroring the server `@Roles`. **⚑ DECISIONS vs the brief (all flagged, none silent).** (1) **Endpoint decomposition.** The brief listed both a `qc` (pass/fail) *and* a separate `ready` endpoint — two routes for the one `QC→READY` transition. Folded `ready` into `qc(pass)`: `qc` is the QC-station decision (`pass → READY`, `fail → REWASH`), so there is exactly one verb per real transition and the console shows exactly one legal action per state. All **six** production-region edges are driven: `QUOTE_APPROVED→IN_CLEANING`, `REWASH→IN_CLEANING` (restart — same `start-cleaning` verb, the machine validates from-state), `IN_CLEANING→QC`, `QC→READY`, `QC→REWASH`, `IN_CLEANING→REWASH` (a rewash pulled mid-cleaning). (2) **Strengthened the QC-fail contract.** The brief said the reason was optional; a QC failure now REQUIRES a reason (≥3 chars, Zod `.refine`) and `rewash` requires one too — a rewash decision that sends work backward must be accountable, never a silent bounce (consistent with `holdOrderSchema`). The audited `after` records it. (3) **Order-level ONLY — `Item.status` deliberately untouched.** The brief's Inc-30 verify asserts `Order.status` + a matching `AuditLog` and nothing about per-item status; the `ItemStatus` `IN_CLEANING/QC/READY` denormalization stays out of scope (Inc-31 sets `Item.status = DELIVERED` on delivery). Flagged so the audit chat can decide if it wants the per-item denorm wired later. (4) **Cross-account 403 is NOT reachable here, stated honestly.** All three production roles are ORG-wide, so `requireOrderForWrite`'s scoped read always sees the order — the 403-cross-account branch can't fire for an org-wide principal (an account-confined caller is stopped one layer earlier at the RBAC guard with `forbidden_role`). So the e2e proves the gates that ARE reachable: RBAC 403 (FINANCE/DISPATCHER/RESIDENTIAL_CUSTOMER blocked, CSR/PLANT_OP allowed), cross-org → 404 `order_not_found` (no existence leak), and every illegal jump → 409 `illegal_transition` with the row left untouched. **TESTS.** `production.e2e-spec.ts` (real app/Postgres, minted JWTs, mirrors `orders.e2e`): the happy path asserts each `Order.status` in the DB **and** a matching `STATUS_TRANSITION` AuditLog (JSON-path `after.status`); the rewash branch (`qc fail → REWASH → restart → IN_CLEANING`, incl. fail-without-reason → 400); a mid-cleaning `rewash` (reason-required); illegal jumps → 409 (start-cleaning from QUOTED; qc from IN_CLEANING; send-to-qc from QUOTE_APPROVED); the RBAC + cross-org matrix; and the concurrent-`start-cleaning` race → exactly one 200 / one 409 (the atomic conditional-update guard). `ProductionActions.test.tsx` mirrors `SchedulingCard.test`: the right CTA per status, the correct client call + body (incl. the fail-reason modal), the blank-reason guard, null render for a non-production role or a non-production status, and the 409→"moved on" surface. **VERIFY.** `packages/shared` rebuilt (dist carries the new schemas); api `tsc` clean; web `tsc` clean; `production.e2e` **6/6** + `orders.e2e` **17/17** (twice, `--forceExit` per the known Redis/outbox exit-hang) against the live seeded dev DB. **Not deployed** (`railway up` api+web is the founder's call — console-only, no app rebuild). **Not committed** (code only, per the standing pattern). **Next:** Inc-31 delivery legs (`READY → … → DELIVERED`), which this unblocks. · → packages/shared/src/orders.ts, apps/api/src/orders/{production.service.ts (new), orders.module.ts, orders.controller.ts}, apps/api/test/production.e2e-spec.ts (new), apps/web/{components/order/{ProductionActions.tsx (new), ProductionActions.test.tsx (new)}, components/OrderDetail.tsx, lib/{api-client.ts, rbac.ts}}, CHANGELOG.md, handoffs/2026-07-10-inc30-production-advance.md
- 21:40 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-29 — Deterministic order-item ordering: `Item` gets a `sortOrder` ordinal, the `items` include gets an `ORDER BY`, and the correctness/mispricing risk flagged in Inc-28 §7 is CLOSED. Additive migration + a deterministic backfill, applied and verified on the live dev DB. api unit 338 (unchanged); api e2e 411 → 418/418 (33 suites, run twice back-to-back); `tsc` clean across all packages. Proven live: after a mutation shuffles the rows on disk, the API still returns submitted order — then every proof row removed and the append-only trigger confirmed re-enabled.** **THE BUG (Inc-28 independent audit, now fixed).** `FULL_INCLUDE.items` (`orders.service.ts` — the brief called it `ORDER_INCLUDE`; there is no such symbol) included `items` with **no `orderBy`**, and `Item` had no ordinal column, so an order's items came back in Postgres **heap order**. Heap order is not insertion order: an UPDATE writes a new tuple version at the end of the page's free space, so **an inspected or weighed item moves to the end of the scan** — two reads of the same order could list its items differently, on the customer app, the console and the driver's manifest alike, and an index-based consumer could bind a value to the wrong line (Inc-28 caught a rug's `sqft` landing on a garment; `quotes.build` correctly refused to price it, 422). **⚑ CORRECTIONS to the brief, all checked before writing a line — and all made the fix SAFER, not harder.** (1) The brief said "all items in a booking share one transaction `createdAt`, so there's no tiebreak". **False, and it matters:** Prisma stamps `created_at` **per INSERT statement**, so `book()`'s per-item loop separates them by 2–12 ms. Measured on the dev DB: of 765 orders with items, **744 hold a single item** (trivially exact), **20 multi-item orders have fully DISTINCT timestamps** (the backfill RECOVERS their true submitted order), and **exactly ONE** (`a6000000-…-0101`, a seeded `createMany` of two LINEN items) ties and gets an order IMPOSED by uuid. So the honesty note is the inverse of the brief's fear: the backfill **recovers** real order for 764/765, and I do not describe the one tie as "restored". (2) The brief's backfill `PARTITION BY order_id` ignores that **`Item.orderId` is NULLABLE** — 38 Track-B resident garments have no order. Scoped the backfill to `order_id IS NOT NULL` so those keep the column default (they are read by `resident_id`, ordered by `updatedAt`, bucketed by `garment_status` — ranking them would lump every orderless garment in the org into one meaningless sequence). (3) The brief says quote build + the weigh mapping are "index-fragile consumers". **The SERVER isn't** — `quote.service` keys lines by `itemId`, and `weigh.service` reads by `itemId` or errors on ambiguity; the only index-fragile consumer was the **weigh e2e's test helper** (fixed in Inc-28, and now restored — see below). Reported that honestly rather than inventing a server mispricing path. **THE FIX.** Schema: `Item.sortOrder Int @default(0)` + a composite `@@index([orderId, sortOrder])`. **Verified `Item` carries NO append-only trigger** (those guard CustodyEvent/AuditLog/WeightCapture/LinenMovement/ProcessedStripeEvent — confirmed against the live catalog) so the backfill UPDATE is permitted. Migration `20260710213000_item_sort_order_inc29`: additive `ADD COLUMN … NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (no rewrite failure), then `UPDATE … SET sort_order = ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY order_id ORDER BY created_at, id) - 1 WHERE order_id IS NOT NULL`, then the index. **Verified post-backfill on live:** every order's items form a contiguous 0..n-1 sequence (0 broken), `sort_order` agrees with `(created_at,id)` rank on every row (0 disagreements), all 38 orderless garments at 0, index present. Write path: `book()` sets `sortOrder` = the item's index in the SUBMITTED array (`dto.items.entries()`); the seed's one two-item `createMany` states `sortOrder: 0,1` explicitly rather than leaving it to be imposed; **no intake add-item path exists** (grepped — `orderId` is set once at creation, never reassigned, so the value is write-once-correct). Read path: **one exported `ITEM_ORDER_BY = [{sortOrder},{id}]`** (a single constant, not a literal copied five times — that duplication is exactly how surfaces drift), applied to `FULL_INCLUDE.items` **and** both manifest includes **and** the two custody-writer `item.findMany`s (so the PICKED_UP custody chain is written in the customer's item order); the `id` tiebreak closes the sequence into a total order for the backfilled rows whose timestamps tied. Also gave `FULL_INCLUDE.lines` an `id` tiebreak for the same reason. **TESTS.** New `item-ordering.e2e-spec.ts` (7 tests) does not take the bug on faith: it reads the **physical** order directly via `ORDER BY ctid`, proves it genuinely diverges the moment an item is inspected (`[0]` moves to the end on disk), and proves the API is unmoved — the driver manifest too. Plus backfill-contiguity and garments-untouched assertions. Its teardown disables the CustodyEvent append-only trigger inside a transaction (`ALTER TABLE` is transactional, so a crash rolls the guard back on — never left off) and re-enables it, so it leaves **zero** dev-DB residue, unlike the Inc-27-flagged specs. **⚑ RESTORED the weigh e2e's index mapping** (Inc-28 had rewritten it to map-by-`kind` as a stopgap around this very bug): it maps `order.items[i]` by index again, now CORRECT not lucky, and asserts the returned kinds equal the submitted kinds — so **`weigh.e2e` passing is itself a proof of the guarantee** (an inspection there mutates an item, moving its tuple, yet a later index read still binds the right measurement). No assertion weakened anywhere. **VERIFY.** api `tsc` clean; shared/customer/web `tsc` clean (clients define their own `ItemView` and consume the already-ordered array — nothing downstream needed touching); api unit **338/338**; api e2e **411 → 418/418** (33 suites, +7), run **twice back-to-back** identical, with the CustodyEvent trigger confirmed `enabled` and zero `ITEM ORDER E2E` residue after. customer **173/173**, web **204/204** (one `CostCenters` `act()` flake under full-suite parallelism, reproduced green on re-run, touches no file in this increment). **LIVE (localhost:3111, real dev DB, as the real OWNER/CSR, then fully reverted):** booked a 4-item order `UPHOLSTERY,RUG,BLIND,CARPET` → two reads identical with `sortOrder 0,1,2,3`; inspected item #0 → the **physical `ctid` order shuffled to `RUG,BLIND,CARPET,UPHOLSTERY`** (the exact pre-Inc-29 bug) while the **API stayed `UPHOLSTERY,RUG,BLIND,CARPET`**; proof order + its items/tags/custody deleted (trigger toggled + restored to `O`), whole-DB backfill re-checked contiguous, other agent's `:3000` untouched. **DEPLOY:** the additive column + backfill run via the boot-time `prisma migrate deploy` on the next `railway up` — inside the normal deploy path, not manual DB surgery. Safe to batch with the Inc-28 deploy or ship right after. **Not committed** (code only, per the standing pattern). **Optional tidy NOT done** (out of scope): the pre-existing customer lint failures on `ChatScreen.tsx`/`OrderDetailScreen.tsx` remain (unmodified from HEAD). · → apps/api/prisma/{schema.prisma, migrations/20260710213000_item_sort_order_inc29/ (new), seed.ts}, apps/api/src/orders/{item-order.ts (new), orders.service.ts, intake.service.ts}, apps/api/src/logistics/{manifest.service.ts, route-stop.service.ts}, apps/api/test/{item-ordering.e2e-spec.ts (new), weigh.e2e-spec.ts}, CHANGELOG.md, handoffs/2026-07-10-inc29-item-ordering.md
- 20:52 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-28 — Pickup scheduling: a REAL time-slot picker (Part B), a config-driven availability endpoint (Part A), the owner control that sets the hours (Part C), and the fix that makes the chosen slot actually reach the truck. api unit 277 → 338; api e2e 368 → 411/411 (32 suites, twice back-to-back); web 178 → 204; customer 144 → 173. `tsc` clean across all 7 packages. Proven live against real Postgres/PostGIS/Redis end-to-end, then every row removed and `Organization.settings` restored byte-for-byte.** **⚑ THE BRIEF'S CENTRAL CLAIM WAS FALSE — checked before writing a line.** It stated the chosen window "flows end-to-end" because "downstream consumes windows already (`RouteStop.timeWindow`, the optimizer, on-time analytics)". It does not. There are exactly **two** `routeStop.create` call sites: `recurring/standing-order-generation.service.ts:296` (generated orders, from the standing order's own window) and `logistics/route.service.ts:115`, the dispatcher's `POST /routes/:id/stops`, which wrote `timeWindow: dto.timeWindow ?? Prisma.JsonNull` and **never read the order's `scheduledPickupWindow`**. So a customer-booked window died on the `Order` row: `OptimizerService` sorted on a null `windowStartMs` and `onTimePercent` discarded the stop as unmeasurable. Shipping a "real" picker on top of that would have been a **decorative** picker — precisely what the honesty firewall forbids. Founder authorised the fix. **`route.service.ts` now inherits**: an added stop with no explicit `dto.timeWindow` takes the order's window (PICKUP/BOTH → `scheduledPickupWindow`, DELIVERY → `scheduledDeliveryWindow`); an explicit dispatcher window still wins (it is the newer fact); a `isStoredWindow()` guard means a JSON-`null` literal (what `book()` writes for a windowless order) or a hand-edited row is never copied onto a stop as a window-shaped hole. **⚑ This immediately broke `logistics.e2e`'s optimizer test — which is the change working.** That test's own comment read "no time windows → pure distance reorder", but `bookOrder()` always sent `pickupWindow: win()`; it passed only because the stop silently dropped it. Window start is a HARD ordering key ahead of distance, and the three `bookOrder()` calls' windows differ by the milliseconds between them, which pinned the original sequence and hid the reorder entirely. Fixed by making the test's stated intent true (book windowless orders), not by weakening the assertion. **PART C first, because config without a control is a stub.** Verified the premise: `Organization.settings` had **zero readers** anywhere in the codebase and `billing_config` **zero writers** — `taxRate` is settable only at bootstrap via `BOOTSTRAP_TAX_RATE`, and `minOrderCents` has a reader and **no writer at all**. So "founder-tunable" meant "editable with a SQL client". New **`GET`/`PATCH /v1/admin/settings/schedule`** (OWNER-gated, org-scoped, audited `PICKUP_SCHEDULE_UPDATED` with `before`/`after` carrying the `source`, so the log distinguishes "changed their hours" from "set hours for the first time, replacing a default they never chose"). The schedule lives at **`Organization.settings.schedule`, not `billing_config.schedule`** as the brief proposed — `settings` already holds `{region, currency, tz}`, operating hours are not a billing concern, and slot generation needs exactly that `tz`. This gives `settings` its **first reader and its first writer**. `PATCH` is a full replacement, merged so bootstrap's sibling keys survive. **PART A — availability, honest by construction.** `GET /v1/availability/pickup-slots?date=&siteId=` (customer-scoped roles, mirroring `SitesController.MANAGE`). Windows come from `resolveOrgSchedule()` — **one reader shared by the endpoint and the console**, because two readers is how the console comes to show hours the endpoint does not serve. **Capacity is a real count or nothing**: with `maxPerSlot: null` (the default — Blue Bee has no measured per-slot truck capacity, so shipping a number would invent scarcity) the query does not even run, `capacityChecked: false` and `remaining: null` throughout; with a cap set, one `jsonb` overlap query per day buckets real orders in `REQUESTED|SCHEDULED_PICKUP|PICKUP_EN_ROUTE` (a collected order releases its slot; `ON_HOLD` never holds one) and a met cap is `reason: 'full'`. The `jsonb_typeof(...) = 'object'` guard matters: `book()` writes `Prisma.JsonNull` — a JSON `null` **literal**, not SQL NULL — so `IS NOT NULL` alone would feed those rows to the timestamp cast. **`scheduleSource: 'default'`** labels the built-in hours as built-in, everywhere they surface: the customer app prints a note, the console shows an amber "Using default hours" badge. Three-state shape deliberately mirrors Inc-26's `SiteServiceability.checked`. `siteId` is row-scoped (another account's, or a **deactivated** address → 404 `availability.site_not_found`, never 403 — no existence leak) and feeds `resolveScheduleForSite()`, the documented **zone seam**; `ServiceZone` carries no hours/capacity column today, so it returns the org schedule unchanged. **No fake column was added** — the repo already carries one such ghost (`Site.serviceWindows`: seeded, read nowhere). Nothing geo-blocks. **DST correctness, and where it came from.** `owner-kpi.ts` privately owned the only wall-clock↔UTC conversion in the codebase; it is now `common/tz.ts`, shared, and `businessDayStart` is implemented on it. **⚑ Its single-pass conversion was subtly wrong**: it read the zone offset at `now`, not at the resolved instant, so on the spring-forward day "09:00 Toronto" resolved an hour late. `zonedWallTimeToUtc` runs two passes; a spec pins `2026-03-08 09:00 → 13:00Z`, not `14:00Z`. **⚑ And a semantic bug my own unit test caught:** with `leadTimeHours: 0`, a window that had *started but not ended* was reported `reason: 'lead_time'` — "we need more notice" when no notice is required. The rule is now stated on the window's START: `'past'` = the start has passed (under way, or over — to a customer the same fact); `'lead_time'` = the start is still ahead but sooner than the lead; at zero lead `'lead_time'` is unreachable. **PART B — the picker.** `tomorrowMorningWindow()` is **deleted**. It fabricated tomorrow 09:00–12:00 on the *device's* calendar, behind a single Switch, with no reference to the org's hours, timezone, lead time or capacity. `BookScreen` now renders a day strip straight from the server's `bookableDates` (the app never decides which days are open — a phone in another timezone would offer a Sunday the plant is shut) and that day's windows from `slots`. **`PickupSlot` carries `startLocal`/`endLocal`** (the founder's `HH:MM`) alongside the ISO instants, and the app displays those: a customer in Vancouver must see "9:00 AM", not their phone's arithmetically-true, operationally-useless "6:00 AM" — and Hermes' partial `Intl` cannot be trusted with a `timeZone` on Android anyway, so `format.ts`'s new helpers are pure string/index math. Unavailable slots are **disabled and say why** from the server's `reason`, never hidden. A `remaining` count renders **only** when `capacityChecked` is true. A fully-booked or closed day says so; **submit is blocked until a real slot is chosen** (and a guard in `submit()` means no future label refactor can smuggle a booking through). The availability read is BLOCKING and gated on the address load, so it fires **once**, with the resolved `siteId`, instead of twice against `undefined`. **⚑ A latent bug in `orders.service.ts` that my e2e's `Item` deletions exposed, and which I did NOT fix.** `ORDER_INCLUDE` gives `lines`, `inspections`, `photos`, `custodyEvents`, `quotes` and `routeStops` an explicit `orderBy` — but **not `items`**. Postgres therefore returns them in heap order, and the whole booking runs in one transaction so every `Item.createdAt` is the same transaction timestamp with no tiebreak. `weigh.e2e-spec.ts` mapped measurements to items **by index** on the comment "Book preserves item order"; when my teardown's deletions changed the heap layout the order came back rug-first, the rug's `{sqft:40}` landed on the wash-and-fold garment, and `quotes.build` **correctly** refused to price the unmeasured rug line (422). The tests had been passing on luck of physical row order. Fixed the test (map by `kind`, return ids in request order); **flagged the missing `orderBy`** — two reads of the same order can list its items differently, on every surface — because giving `items` a deterministic order is a change every consumer sees and deserves its own increment. **LIVE PROOF (localhost:3111 against the real dev DB, as the real principals, then fully reverted).** OWNER → `GET /admin/settings/schedule` = the labelled `source: "default"`; customer Priya → `GET /availability/pickup-slots` at 11:09 EDT correctly returns Morning `past` (started), Afternoon `lead_time` (51 min out, 2 h required), Evening bookable, with `09:00 EDT = 13:00Z`; OWNER **403** on customer availability, customer **403** on the schedule, anon **401**; `PATCH` rejects overlapping windows (`"windows overlap: A (09:00–13:00) and B (12:00–15:00)"`) and a **zero cap** (`0` ≠ "no cap"); OWNER saves `Early bird 07:00–10:00` + `Evening 17:00–20:00`, lead 1 h, horizon 4 d, **cap 1**, closed Sundays → customer instantly sees `scheduleSource: "org"`, Sunday `closed: true`, `2026-07-16` → **400 `availability.date_out_of_range`**; Priya books the offered `Early bird` slot → the order stores **exactly** those instants → the same slot returns `available: false, remaining: 0, reason: "full"` while `Evening` stays open; the dispatcher adds a stop with **no** `timeWindow` → **`{"start":"2026-07-13T11:00:00.000Z","end":"2026-07-13T14:00:00.000Z"}`**, the customer's window, on the stop the driver runs. Exactly **one** `PICKUP_SCHEDULE_UPDATED` audit row, actor "Demo Owner", `before.source: default → after.source: org`. Order/stop/route/items/tags deleted and `settings` restored to `{"tz":"America/Toronto","region":"GTA","currency":"CAD"}`; the other agent's API on `:3000` untouched. **Founder input still open (non-blocking):** the real Blue Bee pickup hours + whether to enforce a per-slot cap. Deliberately a **separate `AdminCustomersController`** with a class-level `@Roles(OWNER, CSR)` rather than three more methods on `AdminController` (`@Roles(OWNER)`): `RolesGuard` uses `getAllAndOverride`, so a method-level widen *would* work — but leaning on decorator precedence to widen a **PII gate** is exactly what breaks silently when someone reorders decorators. Every other `/v1/admin` route stays OWNER-only. **Reuses, never re-implements:** `scopedPrisma` (org boundary), `ServiceZonesService.isServiceable` (the *same* PostGIS `ST_Covers` gate the booking path uses — a second coverage implementation is how the console comes to disagree with the truck), `maskPushToken`, `AuditService`, and — after the tests caught me hand-rolling both — `decimalMoney` + `parseOrderTotals` from `orders/money.ts`. **⚑ Two REAL bugs my own tests caught, both now fixed:** `Decimal.toString()` drops the trailing zero, so an invoice of `158.20` was crossing the wire as `"158.2"` and would have rendered **"$158.2"** in the console (`money.ts` says so in a comment: every `Decimal(12,2)` must pass through `decimalMoney`); and my bespoke `Order.totals` parser duplicated `parseOrderTotals`, the single source of truth for "is this order billable and for how much" — two parsers is how the console and the invoice engine drift. **The honesty firewall, encoded in the contract and rendered in the UI.** (1) `Order` has **no `userId`** — orders/invoices/payments hang off the ACCOUNT. So every such field is named `account*` (`accountOrderCount`, `lastAccountOrderAt`), the table header reads **"Account orders"**, and the card subtitle says "Orders on this customer's account". (2) Per-order attribution comes only from `Order.createdBy`: `bookedByThisCustomer` is `true`/`false`/**`null`**, and `null` (the column is unset on older rows) renders as an em dash — **never "Staff"**, a claim the data cannot support. (3) Coverage is the same three-state answer as Inc-26: the org has **zero active service zones**, so the gate is inert and every address reports `checked: false` → the badge says **"Coverage not checked"**, never "Outside service area"; an address with no point says "No map pin". (4) `notifications.configured: false` means the customer never opened the settings and is on server defaults — the card says exactly that instead of presenting a default as a choice. (5) An unpriced order shows an em dash, **never `$0.00`**. (6) **Chat is absent by design** — the driver↔customer thread is staff-invisible, console moderation is a separate decision, so the aggregate carries no chat field to accidentally render, and the page names the boundary in a footnote. **Confinement.** A **staff user's id → 404** (asserted, and asserted that the body does not contain their name): this route reads consumer PII and must never become a staff-PII read. An `ACCOUNT_ADMIN` → 404. Cross-org → 404, both directions. A malformed id → 400, not an unbounded scan. `limit=0|999` and a malformed cursor → 400; blank `?search=&cursor=` → 200 (a blank is "absent", not "invalid"). **Accountability (§8.7).** Opening one 360 writes exactly one immutable `CUSTOMER_360_VIEWED` AuditLog row naming the staff actor; **listing writes none** (a directory read is not a PII drill-down), and a **403 or 404 writes none** (nothing was read). - 18:05 IST · **[BLUE BEE] Inc-26 — Saved addresses (add · manage · pick): the customer onboarding blocker is CLOSED. A customer with zero sites can now type an address, have the SERVER geocode it against a real provider, and book. Full vertical slice, no stubs. Proven live against the real DB + real OpenStreetMap, plus 28 new e2e / 21 new api-unit / 36 new customer tests.** **⚑ THE BRIEF'S CENTRAL PREMISE WAS FALSE — checked before writing a line.** It said to geocode "via Google Maps Platform (already the stack's maps/geo provider)". It is not: `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` is **empty** in `apps/api/.env`, **no Google SDK is a dependency** of this repo, and there was **zero geocoding code** anywhere in the API. Google Maps is aspirational in `CLAUDE.md`/`.env.example` and was never provisioned — meanwhile `apps/web` already renders **Leaflet + OpenStreetMap**. Surfaced to the founder, who chose **Nominatim (OSM)**. **(1) The geocoder seam.** New `GEOCODER_PROVIDER` DI token + `GeocoderProvider` interface + `NominatimGeocoder` (keyless; the same data source as the map the customer already sees), bound by the `@Global` `GeocodingModule` and configured by `config/geocoding.config.ts` (`GEOCODER_PROVIDER=nominatim|google`). **Deliberately asymmetric with `PaymentProvider`: there is NO local/offline geocoder.** A synthetic payment intent is inert; a synthetic COORDINATE routes a truck to the wrong door and poisons the serviceability gate — so the seam has exactly two honest outcomes, a real point or a thrown `GeocodeError`. `not_found` (the upstream answered "no such address") → **422 `site.geocode_failed`, and NOTHING is written**; `unavailable` (timeout/500/malformed body) → **503 `site.geocode_unavailable`**, transient and retryable. Collapsing those two would either tell a customer their real address doesn't exist, or invite an infinite retry against a typo. OSM's usage policy is *enforced, not documented*: an identifying `User-Agent` on every call, and a single-flight promise-chain throttle spacing outbound calls ≥ `NOMINATIM_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (1100 ms), so N concurrent saves queue instead of bursting the platform's IP into a ban. Selecting `google` without a key **throws at boot** rather than silently resolving addresses against a different upstream than the operator intended. **(2) Schema (additive, reversible).** Migration `20260710120000_saved_addresses_inc26`: `Site += unit, buzzer, notes` (TEXT NULL), `is_default` (NOT NULL DEFAULT false), `active` (NOT NULL DEFAULT true). **`Site.name` doubles as the customer-facing label** ("Home"/"Work") — no parallel `label` column, so `BookScreen`'s `s.name` read keeps working. "At most one default per account" is a **DATABASE invariant, not an app convention**: hand-authored partial unique index `Site_one_default_per_account ON (account_id) WHERE is_default AND active` (**proven in psql**: two active defaults → `duplicate key violates …`; an active default + a deactivated one coexist, so removing yesterday's default never blocks promoting today's). Plus a hand-authored `Site_geo_gist` (Prisma cannot index an `Unsupported` geography column), which also accelerates the Inc-15 building-match `ST_DWithin`. **(3) Endpoints.** `POST /v1/sites` · `PATCH /v1/sites/:id` · `POST /v1/sites/:id:deactivate` — idempotency-keyed, `@Roles(RESIDENTIAL_CUSTOMER, ACCOUNT_ADMIN, ACCOUNT_STAFF)` (unlike GET `/sites` on `CatalogController`, which has no role gate). **⚑ The escaped-colon route was verified empirically, and my first test was WRONG:** the hoisted `express` is **5.2.1 / path-to-regexp 3.3.0**, but `@nestjs/platform-express` resolves its OWN nested **express 4.22.1 / path-to-regexp 0.1.13** — retested against the real one, `@Post(':id\\:deactivate')` matches and captures the bare UUID (`id` ≠ `<uuid>:deactivate`). **No collision** with `FacilityLinenController`'s `@Controller('sites')`: every FL route is a 3-segment `sites/:siteId/<noun>`; mine are 1- and 2-segment. Confirmed in the boot log's route table. **(4) Serviceability is a THREE-state answer, not a boolean** — encoded in the type (`SiteServiceability { checked, serviceable, coverageRequestId }`). The org currently has **ZERO active zones** (both seeded ones are literally named "(redraw me)" and are inactive), so the geo-gate is **inert** — and an inert gate is *not evidence of coverage*. `checked: false` ⇒ the app claims **nothing**. Only `checked && serviceable` prints "we serve this address"; only a non-null `coverageRequestId` prints "we've noted your address". An out-of-area address is still SAVED, and its demand captured exactly as a blocked booking does (`context.source = 'saved_address'`). **(5) Soft delete, scoped deliberately.** "Remove" flips `active` (a past `Order`/`RouteStop` references the row). `catalog.listSites` now filters `active: true` and sorts default-first (so `sites[0]` is the right pre-selection); ops/driver/billing/corporate reads intentionally still resolve a deactivated site, which is what keeps a historical order rendering the address it was actually collected from. **Found and closed a hole this opened:** `OrdersService.book()` resolved `siteId` with **no `active` filter**, so a stale client could keep booking pickups at a deleted address → now `active: true`. Deactivating the default **promotes the oldest remaining active site** (the server decides; the app re-fetches rather than guessing) and clears `is_default` so the partial index frees. **(6) 403 vs 404 — a deliberate, flagged deviation from the brief.** The brief demanded "another customer gets **403**". The codebase convention (`accounts-portal.e2e-spec.ts:212/221`) is **403 = wrong ROLE**, **404 = out-of-scope row id** — so a probe cannot use the status code to prove another customer's address exists. I implemented **404 for cross-account** (scopedPrisma resolves it to null) + **403 for wrong role**, and tested both, rather than silently weakening row-scoping to satisfy the letter of the brief. **(7) Customer app.** `customer-client` += `createSite`/`updateSite`/`deactivateSite`; `ApiError` += `isGeocodeFailed` (422 — the user fixes it) / `isGeocodeUnavailable` (503 — retry), two paths the app must never conflate. New `AddressFormScreen` (a 422 lands on the address FIELD, not a generic red box; a 503 KEEPS the idempotency key, because the first attempt may have landed) + `AddressesScreen` (list · edit · set-default · two-step remove) + a Settings → Addresses row. **`BookScreen`: the zero-state IS the CTA** — previously `{sites.length > 0 ? <Card/> : null}` hid the whole card, so a new customer saw nothing and could never add an address; and submit was live with **no `siteId`**, now gated ("Add an address to book"). Book re-reads the picker on `focus` and pre-selects the address just saved. **⚑ `useFocusEffect` broke all 8 existing BookScreen tests** (it needs a `NavigationContainer` in the tree) — swapped to the prop-level `navigation.addListener('focus')`, which is the documented API and unit-testable; `SettingsScreen`'s `useNavigation()` needed the hook stubbed for the same reason. **No map-pin picker shipped, on purpose:** `apps/customer/app.json` has no `android.config.googleMaps.apiKey` (the parked Tier-1 item), so a picker would render a blank tile on Android. The API already accepts a `geo` pin, so the control drops in unchanged when that key lands. **PROOF (live, real Nominatim, real PostGIS, as the real ACCOUNT-scoped customer).** `100 Queen St W, Toronto` → geocoded to **(-79.3840055, 43.6536032)** = Toronto City Hall; a nonsense address → **422 + zero rows written** (row count identical before/after); the same `Idempotency-Key` twice → **one row, same id**; promote-default → exactly 1 active default; deactivate the default → next site promoted, still exactly 1; with a temporary active downtown zone, `200 University Ave` → `{checked:true, serviceable:true}` and `111 Wellington St, Ottawa` → `{checked:true, serviceable:false, coverageRequestId:…}` + exactly one `CoverageRequest`. Temp zone and every smoke row deleted afterwards; the founder's inactive "(redraw me)" zones untouched. **Regression:** api unit **240→261**; api e2e **+28** (new `sites.e2e-spec.ts`, injecting a fake geocoder through a new `createTestApp(overrides)` so no test ever touches OSM); customer **108→144** (17→20 suites); api/customer/shared `tsc` all clean. Full api e2e = **336/340**. **⚑ The 4 failures are PRE-EXISTING and NOT mine — proven by `git stash`ing my entire diff and reproducing them identically on the clean tree.** Root-caused: **`seed.ts:467` and `seed.ts:776` assign the SAME uuid `e1000000-…-010` to two different users** — "Demo Test Owner (simple login)" (OWNER/ORG) and "Demo Care-Home Admin" (ACCOUNT_ADMIN). The owner seeds first, so the care-home upsert matches on `id` and its `update: { phone: '+14165550030' }` hands the care-home admin's phone to an **org-wide OWNER**, which keeps its OWNER grant. `care-home-linen.e2e-spec.ts` then mints an org-wide token for `careAdmin` and its 4 tenancy/RBAC assertions collapse (404→200, 403→201). **NOT fixed here** — `+14165550030` may be the founder's deliberate "simple login", so reassigning that phone is a founder decision, not a silent edit. **Not deployed** (code only; `railway up` + the next EAS build are the founder's call). · → packages/shared/src/{sites.ts (new), catalog.ts, index.ts}, packages/api-client/src/problem.ts, apps/api/prisma/{schema.prisma, migrations/20260710120000_saved_addresses_inc26/}, apps/api/src/{geocoding/ (new: geocoder.provider.ts, nominatim.geocoder.ts, nominatim.geocoder.spec.ts, geocoding.module.ts), config/{geocoding.config.ts (new), geocoding.config.spec.ts (new)}, sites/ (new: sites.service.ts, sites.controller.ts, sites.module.ts), catalog/catalog.service.ts, orders/orders.service.ts, app.module.ts}, apps/api/test/{sites.e2e-spec.ts (new), app.factory.ts}, apps/customer/{App.tsx, src/navigation.ts, src/api/customer-client.ts, src/addresses/ (new: address.ts, address.test.ts), src/screens/{AddressFormScreen.tsx (new), AddressesScreen.tsx (new), AddressFormScreen.test.tsx (new), AddressesScreen.test.tsx (new), BookScreen.tsx, BookScreen.test.tsx, SettingsScreen.tsx, SettingsScreen.test.tsx}}, .env.example, handoffs/2026-07-10-inc26-saved-addresses.md
